- **How to implement:** `clearing_price = ceiling_price × (1 − discount)`, with discount ≥ 0.34 for HV and ≥ 0.18 for LV as a P50 floor, and a P90 case using the observed clearing distribution (e.g. NGED SU-HV autumn 2025 median £91.93/MWh against ceilings up to £9,000/MWh). Store ceiling and clear as separate fields so the two are never conflated.
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Flexibility Market Insights Report', June 2025, p.7, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1184
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'NGED DSO - Revenue Estimator September 2025 V2.0', Overview notes ('upper bound of potential earnings'), 2025, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1176
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Pricing Strategy', Version 1.2, October 2022, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1112

#### D-122 · MUST · Represent service windows as hard time masks in the half-hourly MILP, and constrain state of charge so the battery can actually deliver the contracted MW for the required minimum run time at any point inside an accepted window.

- **Why:** Awards are made per Delivery Period within a Capacity Requirement Window (NGED uses half-hour or four-hour delivery periods; UKPN uses e.g. 16:30–19:30 weekdays, Nov–Mar). Every DNO requires a minimum delivery duration of 30 minutes. Availability is only payable inside accepted windows, and a battery that has arbitraged its energy away cannot deliver.
- **Error if omitted:** The optimiser will double-count energy — selling the same MWh into wholesale/BM and into the DNO window — and the underwriting model will book availability revenue the asset was not physically able to stand behind, exposing it to the MPF clawback and to derating in future rounds.
- **How to implement:** For each accepted window w with delivery periods T_w and contracted capacity CC: enforce `discharge_power[t] ≤ P_max` and `SoC[t] ≥ CC × min_run_time_hours / η_dis` for all t ∈ T_w (a reserve-headroom constraint), plus `Σ_{t∈event} discharge[t] ≥ CC × duration` when a utilisation binary u_e = 1. Minimum run time = 30 min (0.5 h) for NGED, UKPN and SSEN. Use half-hourly resolution as the modelling floor but note settlement may be minute-by-minute.
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Guidance for Electricity Distribution Flexibility Service Providers', March 2026, Table 2.1 and §5.2, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1121
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Participation Guidance V1.2', October 2024, §3.2, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2024/10/Participation-Guidance-V1.2.pdf
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Competition Data – Summer 2026 Tender V2' (Window Start/Window End, Service Days), 2026, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2026/07/Competition-Data-Summer-2026-Tender-V2.xlsx

#### D-123 · MUST · Cap the volume the model can sell into a zone at that zone's published MW requirement, and model the requirement as varying by season and delivery year.

- **Why:** The DNO clears the stack at its required volume; offers beyond it are rejected. UKPN's Summer-2026 HV Demand zone requirements have a median of 0.90 MW (p75 2.00 MW, max 24.54 MW), and NGED's SAOU_DA HV zones seek a median of ~3.3 MW. A 5 MW battery in a 0.9 MW zone can sell at most 0.9 MW, and less if others bid.
- **Error if omitted:** Over-statement proportional to (battery MW ÷ zone requirement MW). For a 5 MW asset in a median UKPN HV zone the naive error is 5.6×.
- **How to implement:** `contracted_MW = min(battery_available_MW, zone_requirement_MW[zone, season, year] × expected_share)`, where expected_share reflects competing bidders. Ingest UKPN 'Total MW Requirement' per zone-season, NGED 'Minimum/Maximum Trade Required Capacity', SSEN 'Peak Capacity Required'.
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Competition Data – Summer 2026 Tender V2', Competition Data TR14 sheet, 2026, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2026/07/Competition-Data-Summer-2026-Tender-V2.xlsx
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'NGED DSO - Revenue Estimator September 2025 V2.0', HV LT Trade Parameters sheet, 2025, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1176
- *Source:* Scottish and Southern Electricity Networks, 'SLC31E — SSEN 2025-26 Flexibility Services Procurement Report', April 2026, Table 3, https://www.ssen.co.uk/globalassets/our-services/flexibility-services-document-library/slc31e-reports--statements/slc31e--ssen-2025-26-flexibility-services-procurement-report.pdf

#### D-124 · MUST · Enforce minimum offer size and minimum technical requirements as eligibility gates, per DNO.

- **Why:** UKPN requires flexible capacity of at least 10 kW per zone (aggregable across DER in the same zone) sustainable for 30 minutes, and rejects bids below 10 kW. SSEN sets a 2.5 kW minimum offer threshold for SU markets and 10 kW for VAOU, OU and SAOU. NGED sets no capacity threshold but requires the asset or group to deliver for at least 30 minutes and to be energised at registration.
- **Error if omitted:** For a 0.1–5 MW C&I BESS these gates rarely bind, but they do bind for the pilot/partial-capacity cases the model will be asked to run, and they bind for any assumption that a battery can bid sub-30-minute duration.
- **How to implement:** `eligible = (offered_kW ≥ min_offer_kW[dno, product]) and (deliverable_duration_h ≥ 0.5) and asset_energised`. min_offer_kW: UKPN 10; SSEN 2.5 (SU) / 10 (VAOU, OU, SAOU); NGED 0.
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Participation Guidance V1.2', October 2024, §3.2 and 'Long-term Awards', https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2024/10/Participation-Guidance-V1.2.pdf
- *Source:* Scottish and Southern Electricity Networks, 'Assessment of Bids and Offers in SSEN', V2.1, April 2026, §2.1, https://www.ssen.co.uk/globalassets/our-services/flexibility-services-document-library/service-documentation/assessment-of-bids-in-ssen-v2.1-apr-2026.pdf
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Guidance for Electricity Distribution Flexibility Service Providers', March 2026, Table 2.1 and §3.2, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1121

#### D-125 · MUST · Model service direction correctly and separately: demand-constrained zones need Demand Turn Down / Generation Turn Up (discharge), generation-constrained zones need Demand Turn Up / Generation Turn Down (charge). A BTM battery can serve both but not simultaneously, and the baseline differs by direction.

- **Why:** NGED's trade opportunities carry an explicit 'Service Direction' field (DTD/GTU or DTU/GTD); its baseline workbook gives different baselines for the DTU/GTD product codes (SU_HV_DTUGTD, SAOU_DA_DTUGTD). UKPN: 'In demand constrained zones, the required direction is reduced imports or increased exports. In generation constrained zones, the required direction is increased imports or reduced exports.' NGED launched Demand Turn Up zones in 2024/25 and a network-wide 'Responder' DTU trial for Sept 2026–Mar 2027.
- **Error if omitted:** Modelling a discharge-only battery against a generation-constrained zone books revenue the asset cannot earn; ignoring DTU opportunities omits a genuine revenue line whose marginal cost is negative (paid to charge). NGED's revenue estimator note is explicit: 'Turn Down and Turn Up will need to be entered separately.'
- **How to implement:** Separate decision variables and separate contracts per direction; `direction ∈ {DTD_GTU, DTU_GTD}`; enforce mutual exclusivity within a settlement period via a binary. For DTU, the delivered quantity is increased import (charging), settled against the direction-specific baseline.
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Guidance for Electricity Distribution Flexibility Service Providers', March 2026, Table 5.1, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1121
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Participation Guidance V1.2', October 2024, §3.1, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2024/10/Participation-Guidance-V1.2.pdf
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'NGED DSO - Responder Fact Sheet', July 2026 V1.1, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1199
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'NGED DSO - Revenue Estimator September 2025 V2.0', Overview notes, 2025, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1176

#### D-126 · MUST · For SAOU-type contracts, model the availability payment and the utilisation payment as separately-won: winning availability does NOT guarantee winning utilisation, because utilisation is cleared day-ahead against all other day-ahead bids.

- **Why:** UKPN: 'Utilisation of SAOU contracts is instructed day ahead. This means the utilisation portion of the bid is cleared along with the other Day-Ahead bids... The availability portion of the contract will still be paid according to the settlement rules regardless of whether the utilisation was awarded.' NGED has introduced a Joint Utilisation Competition allowing long-term contract holders to re-bid a lower utilisation price to stay competitive against short-term bids.
- **Error if omitted:** Over-statement of utilisation revenue on long-term SAOU contracts. A model that assumes the long-term utilisation price is always paid when the DNO needs energy will over-state whenever a cheaper day-ahead bid clears first.
- **How to implement:** Two coupled probabilities: `P(availability_award)` at tender, and `P(utilisation_clear | availability_award, utilisation_price)` at day-ahead. Expected utilisation revenue = utilisation_price × contracted_MW × availability_hours × utilisation_rate × P(utilisation_clear). Allow the optimiser to re-bid the utilisation price down to marginal opportunity cost (the JUC mechanic) but not below.
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Participation Guidance V1.2', October 2024, §2.4, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2024/10/Participation-Guidance-V1.2.pdf
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Guidance for Electricity Distribution Flexibility Service Providers', March 2026, §5.5.3 Joint Utilisation Competition, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1121
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Distribution Flexibility Services Procurement Statement 2025/26', April 2025, §6.1, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/933

#### D-127 · MUST · Bid the DNO utilisation price at or above the asset's opportunity cost in the co-optimised stack, and co-optimise DNO windows against wholesale, BM and NESO service revenue rather than adding DNO revenue on top.

- **Why:** DNO service windows (typically weekday evening peaks, 16:00–20:00 winter) coincide precisely with the highest-value wholesale and BM periods. SSEN's bid evaluation converts every bid to a single comparator price: Total Price (£/MWh) = Availability Price + (Utilisation Price × Utilisation Weighting Factor), so a high utilisation price weighted by a low expected call rate can still win. The economically correct bid is one that recovers foregone wholesale/BM margin at the expected call rate.
- **Error if omitted:** Either (a) additive stacking, which over-states total revenue by the value of the displaced wholesale/BM trades in the same periods, or (b) a bid price set below opportunity cost, which wins contracts that destroy value. Both errors are largest in exactly the high-price winter evening periods that drive the P50.
- **How to implement:** Single MILP over the year with one energy balance and one power balance; DNO delivery is a constraint on that balance, not a parallel revenue stream. Objective adds `Σ_t (util_price × delivered_MWh[t] × PM[t]) + Σ_w (avail_price × CC × hours_w × MPF)` while the same MWh is unavailable to wholesale/BM. Set the bid price so that `util_price × utilisation_rate + avail_price ≥ E[foregone wholesale/BM margin per available hour]`.
- *Source:* Scottish and Southern Electricity Networks, 'Assessment of Bids and Offers in SSEN', V2.1, April 2026, §2.2, https://www.ssen.co.uk/globalassets/our-services/flexibility-services-document-library/service-documentation/assessment-of-bids-in-ssen-v2.1-apr-2026.pdf
- *Source:* Cornwall Insight for National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Revenue Stacking for Flexibility', December 2023, §3.1–3.2, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1150
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Competition Data – Summer 2026 Tender V2' (Window Start/End showing evening-peak windows), 2026, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2026/07/Competition-Data-Summer-2026-Tender-V2.xlsx

#### D-128 · MUST · Model stacking with NESO services as permitted-but-operationally-constrained: DNO contracts carry no exclusivity, but delivery conflicts must be resolved period by period.

- **Why:** NGED states its 'flexibility service contracts are deliberately designed without exclusivity clauses and fully stackable... not restricted from entering other markets for the same or different settlement period', and complies with the Market Facilitator's Revenue Stacking Requirements rule. NGED moved its long-term HV product from a 15-minute dispatch notice to Day-Ahead in 2024 specifically 'to move the dispatch notice ahead of NESO's Balancing Mechanism gate closure'. NGED allows stacking between HV and LV services and where 11 kV/33 kV HV zones nest inside 132 kV GSP/BSP zones.
- **Error if omitted:** Under-statement of achievable revenue if stacking is assumed prohibited; over-statement if stacking is assumed frictionless and the same MW is sold twice in the same direction and period.
- **How to implement:** Classify every stack as co-delivery (same MW, same time, same direction — allowed only where both services accept it), splitting (different MW, same time) or jumping (same asset, different times). Enforce `Σ_services committed_MW[t] ≤ P_max` and a single direction per period. Encode the day-ahead notice timing (NGED SAOU_DA and UKPN DA-SU notify by 13:30 D-1 / 15:00 D-1) so BM and wholesale positions can be set after the DSO outcome is known.
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Unlocking Value Through Revenue Stacking: National Grid DSO's Approach', March 2026, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1198
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Guidance for Electricity Distribution Flexibility Service Providers', March 2026, Table 2.1 'Stackability', https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1121
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Participation Guidance V1.2', October 2024, §2.5 and §5.3, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2024/10/Participation-Guidance-V1.2.pdf

#### D-129 · MUST · Encode DNO-specific bidding constraints that restrict portfolio optimisation: one product per portfolio (UKPN), all-or-nothing bids, whole-window and whole-season bidding, and exclusion from the short-term stack if a long-term availability instruction was rejected.

- **Why:** UKPN: 'For each portfolio, the provider may choose to submit an offer for either, but not both of, Long-term Scheduled Utilisation or Scheduled Availability Operational Utilisation'; 'variations within the bid are treated as all-or-nothing'; 'bids must be for whole Service Window'; 'must be for a whole Delivery Season'. SSEN excludes from the stack any provider 'where the provider has rejected an availability instruction from an existing long-term contract that they held in the specific CMZ'.
- **Error if omitted:** The optimiser will construct bid portfolios that cannot be submitted, producing revenue that is unattainable. It will also under-price the option value lost by having to choose one product.
- **How to implement:** Binary product selection per portfolio with `Σ_products y_p ≤ 1`; bid acceptance as a single binary over the whole window/season rather than per delivery period; a penalty/exclusion term if a long-term availability instruction is declined.
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Participation Guidance V1.2', October 2024, 'The following bidding rules will apply', https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2024/10/Participation-Guidance-V1.2.pdf
- *Source:* Scottish and Southern Electricity Networks, 'Assessment of Bids and Offers in SSEN', V2.1, April 2026, §2.2, https://www.ssen.co.uk/globalassets/our-services/flexibility-services-document-library/service-documentation/assessment-of-bids-in-ssen-v2.1-apr-2026.pdf

#### D-130 · MUST · Treat the DNO flexibility contract as short-tenor and terminable, and exclude or heavily haircut it from the DSCR base case and P90 revenue.

- **Why:** The ENA Standard Flexibility Services Agreement gives EITHER party the right to terminate on 90 days' written notice with no cause (paragraph 7.4). NGED's long-term cycle runs annually for the following April–March year, with contracts 'awarded on a minimum one year basis, longer contracts up to four years may also be awarded'. There is no obligation on the DNO to re-tender a zone: the DNOA can conclude that reinforcement is now the better option, closing the zone permanently.
- **Error if omitted:** A lender who assumes 10–15 years of DNO flexibility revenue is underwriting a revenue stream that can be terminated on 90 days' notice and whose underlying need disappears when the network is reinforced. This is a covenant-breaking error, not a rounding error.
- **How to implement:** Contract tenor field with default 1 year (NGED annual cycle), max 4 years (NGED) / 2.5 years (UKPN long-term); DSCR base case includes only contracted years; P90 sets DNO flexibility revenue to zero beyond the current contracted term; a 90-day termination scenario in the sensitivity set. Model zone closure on reinforcement completion using the DNO's Network Development Plan / DNOA deferral horizon.
- *Source:* Energy Networks Association, 'Standard Agreement for Flexibility Services' (ENA May 2025 as published by UK Power Networks), General Terms §7.4, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2023/12/Appendix-1-Flexibility-Services-Standard-Agreement-ENA-May-2025.pdf
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Pricing Strategy', Version 1.2, October 2022, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1112
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Distribution Flexibility Services Procurement Statement 2025/26', April 2025, §5.1 (DNOA decision tree), https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/933

#### D-131 · MUST · Carry the contractual liability and insurance terms into the risk model: £200,000 Distribution Limit per incident, £2,000,000 aggregate cap, exclusion of consequential loss, and a requirement to hold appropriate insurance.

- **Why:** The ENA Standard Agreement defines 'Distribution Limit' as £200,000 (Glossary), caps aggregate liability at £2,000,000 (§10.4), excludes loss of profit/revenue/goodwill and indirect loss (§10.3), and requires the Provider to procure appropriate insurance (§10.5). On termination for material or persistent breach the Company may recover its costs of appointing a replacement provider, capped at the Distribution Limit (§7.9).
- **Error if omitted:** Understatement of downside. A persistent-underdelivery termination creates a recoverable cost exposure up to £200,000 plus loss of the revenue stream, against a typical BTM project equity cheque of £0.5–3m — a material, uninsured-by-default line.
- **How to implement:** Risk register entries with quantified caps: `max_replacement_cost_exposure = £200,000`; `aggregate_liability_cap = £2,000,000`; add an annual insurance premium line to opex. Model a 'persistent under-delivery → termination' scenario.
- *Source:* Energy Networks Association, 'Standard Agreement for Flexibility Services', Common Flexibility Service Terms & Conditions Version 3.0 (as published May 2025), Glossary and §§7.9, 8, 10.1–10.7, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2023/12/Appendix-1-Flexibility-Services-Standard-Agreement-ENA-May-2025.pdf

#### D-132 · MUST · Model the settlement and cash-flow timing: monthly self-billing, a provisional invoice on the 1st of the month, a 14-day query window and 60-day payment terms.

- **Why:** NGED's guidance sets out a provisional invoice on the 1st of each month, 14 days for the FSP to query, self-billed final invoice thereafter, and 'the payment terms for the invoice is 60 days', with 12 billing cycles per year. Metering data must be submitted within 7 working days of operational month end or the invoice will be inaccurate.
- **Error if omitted:** Working-capital requirement is understated by roughly 2.5–3 months of DNO flexibility revenue, which matters for a small BTM project's funding requirement and for DSCR timing in the first year.
- **How to implement:** `cash_receipt_date = month_end + 1 day (provisional) + 14 days (query) + 60 days (terms)`; hold a receivable of ~2.5 months of DNO revenue in working capital. Add a data-submission SLA: metering submitted within 7 working days of month end, else payment slips a cycle.
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Guidance for Electricity Distribution Flexibility Service Providers', March 2026, §6.3 Billing Process and §4.3.2, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1121

#### D-133 · MUST · Model the metering, telemetry and API obligations as a real capex and opex line, and as a technical gate on eligibility.

- **Why:** Every DNO requires half-hourly or minute-by-minute metering data delivered to their platform. NGED requires a Readings API per Meterable Unit, a Dispatch API per Trade Dispatch Group (mandatory for OU products instructed near real time), an optional Stop API, and at least 12 meter readings in the last 7 days to pass metering validation. UKPN requires half-hourly meter data uploaded to Localflex. SSEN uses ElectronConnect.
- **Error if omitted:** Omission of a real cost (metering, comms, aggregator/route-to-market fee, API integration) and a real delivery risk: a site whose metering cannot be proven cannot trade at all. Also omits the choice between direct participation and an aggregator, which carries a revenue share.
- **How to implement:** Capex line for MID-compliant metering + comms; opex line for data/API/platform integration and for aggregator margin if routing through a third party (NGED explicitly notes FSPs may participate via Piclo). Eligibility gate: `metering_proven = (readings_in_last_7_days ≥ 12)`.
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Guidance for Electricity Distribution Flexibility Service Providers', March 2026, §§4.2–4.3, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1121
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Participation Guidance V1.2', October 2024, §3.4 and §6.1, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2024/10/Participation-Guidance-V1.2.pdf
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'NGED DSO - Revenue Estimator September 2025 V2.0', Overview notes (participation via Piclo), 2025, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1176

#### D-134 · MUST · Distinguish DER-level metering from Point-of-Connection metering, and where PoC metering applies, model the site load explicitly at half-hourly resolution — the site's own load variation sits inside the measured delivery.

- **Why:** NGED defines DER Level Metering as covering just the DER with no underlying site load, and Point of Connection metering as inherently incorporating 'any DER and any embedded site demand'. The baseline values differ between the two (e.g. NGED PoC winter household baseline 0.78 kW vs DER-level equivalents). NGED will not allow multiple asset registrations behind a single MPAN if any of them has PoC metering.
- **Error if omitted:** Under PoC metering, a coincident change in the customer's own load is settled as (or against) the battery's delivery. Without half-hourly site load in the model, delivery performance — and therefore the Performance Multiplier and the MPF clawback — cannot be estimated at all.
- **How to implement:** `metering_point ∈ {DER_level, point_of_connection}` as an input; if PoC, `metered_MW[t] = battery_MW[t] − site_load_MW[t] (+ pv_MW[t])` and delivery = metered − baseline. Require half-hourly site load as a hard model input for PoC-metered sites; do not permit a synthetic annual-consumption scaling to stand in for it when computing delivery performance.
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Guidance for Electricity Distribution Flexibility Service Providers', March 2026, §4.1 and Glossary, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1121
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'NGED DSO - Baseline Values August 2025 V1.0' (Asset Level vs Point of Connection blocks), 2025, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1122

#### D-135 · MUST · Apply the DNO's aggregation and registration restrictions: assets are grouped into Meterable Units / Flexible Units within a single zone, Industrial & Commercial demand assets cannot be grouped with others at NGED, and duplicate MPAN registrations are rejected.

- **Why:** NGED: baselining is applied at Meterable Unit level; the grouping table shows 'Industrial or Commercial Demand: No' for grouping while all other categories are 'Yes'; duplicate assets are rejected. UKPN allows aggregation of DERs 'within the same flexibility zone' into a Flexible Unit and resolves contested MPANs on evidence of informed consent.
- **Error if omitted:** The model may assume portfolio aggregation across sites or zones that the DNO will not accept, over-stating both eligibility (minimum size) and diversity benefit. It may also assume a site is available when another provider already holds the MPAN.
- **How to implement:** Aggregation constraint: `same_zone(assets)` required; `if any(asset.type == 'I&C demand') for NGED: group_size == 1`; MPAN uniqueness check against DNO registration. Model the contested-MPAN case (customer already signed to an aggregator) as an eligibility risk with a probability.
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Guidance for Electricity Distribution Flexibility Service Providers', March 2026, §§4.1–4.2, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1121
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Participation Guidance V1.2', October 2024, §3 and §3.3, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2024/10/Participation-Guidance-V1.2.pdf

#### D-136 · MUST · Model past delivery performance as affecting future award probability: DNOs may derate or reject bids on performance grounds and use performance as a tie-break.

- **Why:** UKPN: 'We may derate or reject unit(s), regardless of price, based on past delivery performance, the urgency of capacity needs at the relevant Flexibility Zone and available network options in case of under-delivery.' SSEN: where multiple bids tie at the acceptance cut-off, 'we apply a process that prioritises based on historical delivery performance of FSPs against our requirements.' NGED's service selection prioritises Technical Integrity, Customer Security, Value and Market Resilience in that order.
- **Error if omitted:** A model that treats each year's award as independent of the previous year's delivery misses a compounding downside: one bad delivery year reduces both this year's payment (via MPF and PM) and next year's award probability.
- **How to implement:** Carry a `performance_score` state variable across years, updated from realised MPF; `P(award)[y] = f(bid_price, zone, performance_score[y-1])`. In the P90 case, model a performance-driven exclusion in at least one year.
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Participation Guidance V1.2', October 2024, 'Long-term Awards', https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2024/10/Participation-Guidance-V1.2.pdf
- *Source:* Scottish and Southern Electricity Networks, 'Assessment of Bids and Offers in SSEN', V2.1, April 2026, §2.2, https://www.ssen.co.uk/globalassets/our-services/flexibility-services-document-library/service-documentation/assessment-of-bids-in-ssen-v2.1-apr-2026.pdf
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Distribution Flexibility Services Procurement Statement 2025/26', April 2025, §5.2.1, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/933

#### D-137 · MUST · Reflect the 2024–2026 migration to day-ahead procurement in the model's timing assumptions; a model built on annual or weekly tender cycles alone is now structurally wrong.

- **Why:** UKPN launched day-ahead auctions in April 2024 with notification of dispatch by 13:30 D-1. NGED moved short-term procurement from a week-ahead tender to a day-ahead tender on 31 March 2026, with bid deadline 12:00 D-1 and outcomes from 15:00 the same day; week-ahead procurement ceased with no parallel trading. SSEN introduced day-ahead markets in November 2025. NGED also moved its long-term HV product from a 15-minute dispatch notice to Day-Ahead notice in 2024.
- **Error if omitted:** Wrong optimisation horizon and wrong stacking logic: the day-ahead outcome is now known BEFORE BM gate closure, which changes what can be co-optimised and removes the 'unknown intraday call' friction that older models assume. It also changes the revenue mix from long-term availability toward short-term utilisation-only.
- **How to implement:** Model a day-ahead decision stage at 12:00 D-1 with outcome by 13:30–15:00 D-1, followed by a BM/wholesale positioning stage; long-term tenders remain an annual (NGED, Aug/Sep launch, awards +12 weeks) or bi-annual (UKPN) stage. Do not assume 15-minute-notice dispatch for long-term HV products.
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'NGED DSO - Day-Ahead Flexibility Fact Sheet', March 2026 V1, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1197
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Participation Guidance V1.2', October 2024, §2.5 and §5.3, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2024/10/Participation-Guidance-V1.2.pdf
- *Source:* Scottish and Southern Electricity Networks, 'SLC31E — SSEN 2025-26 Flexibility Services Procurement Report', April 2026, §2.1, https://www.ssen.co.uk/globalassets/our-services/flexibility-services-document-library/slc31e-reports--statements/slc31e--ssen-2025-26-flexibility-services-procurement-report.pdf
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Unlocking Value Through Revenue Stacking', March 2026, item 4, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1198

#### D-138 · MUST · Ingest, and refresh annually, the mandated SLC31E datasets for each relevant DNO, and use them — not vendor marketing — as the calibration source for volumes, prices, award rates and dispatch frequency.

- **Why:** Standard Licence Condition 31E obliges every DNO to publish a forward-looking Distribution Flexibility Services Procurement Statement and a backward-looking Procurement Report with a standard Ofgem data template. The template's Procurement, Procurement-Locational and Dispatch tabs contain per-zone tendered/contracted/unmet MW, tender success rate, dispatched MWh, and per-event availability fee (£/MW/hr), utilisation price (£/MWh), dispatch capacity, duration, notice time and pricing strategy. This is the only source of realised GB DNO flexibility economics at event granularity.
- **Error if omitted:** The model is calibrated on ceilings and indicative ranges rather than realised clears and realised call rates, which is precisely the failure that produces 5–20× revenue over-statement.
- **How to implement:** A data pipeline with per-DNO connectors: NGED Connected Data Portal + Flexible Power downloads (C31E Supporting Data, Weekly/Daily Trade Results, Revenue Estimator, Baseline Values); UKPN Tender Hub Competition Data + Localflex results + Open Data Portal; SSEN Flexibility Services Contract Register + SLC31E supporting data + Open Data Portal; SPEN and Northern Powergrid via Flexible Power. Version and date-stamp every ingest; refuse to run an underwriting case on data past its stated validity date (NGED's Revenue Estimator is explicitly 'Valid Until 2026-03-31').
- *Source:* Ofgem, Standard Licence Condition 31E (Distribution Flexibility Services), as implemented in DNO reporting; see NGED, 'NGED Distribution Flexibility Services Procurement Report 2024-25 V1.0', 2025, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1181
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'C31E Supporting Data 2024-25', 2025, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1180
- *Source:* Scottish and Southern Electricity Networks, 'Flexibility Services Document Library' (SLC31E reports and statements 2021-22 to 2026-27), https://www.ssen.co.uk/about-ssen/dso/flexibility/flexibility-services/flexibility-services-document-library/
- *Source:* UK Power Networks, 'Flexibility Tender Hub / Tender Library', 2026, https://dso.ukpowernetworks.co.uk/flexibility/tender-library

#### D-139 · MUST · Model the DNO flexibility revenue line at P50 and P90 separately, with the P90 case set at or near zero, and never allow it to carry the DSCR.

- **Why:** The stream has at least five multiplicative sources of downside, each individually evidenced: zone existence (most sites have none), award probability (45% of NGED bid rows rejected), clearing price below ceiling (34%/18% at NGED in 2024), utilisation call rate (5% NGED assumption; 14/73 SAOU zones saw zero dispatch in 2024/25), and delivery performance (6% of NGED events failed to respond at all). Compounding even optimistic values for each gives an expected value that is a small fraction of the naive maximum.
- **Error if omitted:** A DSCR that appears to clear on a revenue stream that can go to zero within one tender cycle and is terminable on 90 days' notice. This is the classic lender-facing failure for BTM assets.
- **How to implement:** `E[revenue] = P(zone) × P(award) × clearing_price × contracted_MW × availability_hours × (1 + utilisation_rate × util_price/avail_price) × E[performance_factor]`. P50: use published DNO assumptions. P90: set P(award) and utilisation_rate to their observed lower deciles, and set revenue beyond the current contracted term to zero. Report DNO flexibility as a named 'upside' line, excluded from the base-case DSCR.
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'NGED Distribution Flexibility Services Procurement Report 2024-25 V1.0', Tables 1–3, 2025, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1181
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Flexibility Market Insights Report', June 2025, pp.6–7, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1184
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'C31E Supporting Data 2024-25', Procurement - Locational tab, 2025, https://www.flexiblepower.co.uk/downloads/1180
- *Source:* Energy Networks Association, 'Standard Agreement for Flexibility Services', §7.4, https://d1lf1oz5vvdb9r.cloudfront.net/app/uploads/2023/12/Appendix-1-Flexibility-Services-Standard-Agreement-ENA-May-2025.pdf

#### D-140 · MUST · Model the site's HH net position, not a single wholesale price series. Represent day-ahead auctions as HOURLY blocks (N2EX gate closure 09:50, EPEX 60-min auction 09:20) and only the EPEX 30-minute day-ahead auction at 15:30 D-1 as half-hourly, then intraday auctions (IDA1 gate 17:30 D-1, IDA2 08:00 on-day) and continuous intraday to delivery.

- **Why:** GB day-ahead liquidity is predominantly hourly. A model that optimises against a half-hourly day-ahead price it could not actually have transacted at manufactures spread that does not exist, because it captures within-hour shape that the hourly auction averages away.
- **Error if omitted:** Overstates day-ahead arbitrage revenue. The error is largest for 1-hour assets where a large share of the modelled spread sits inside the hour. Direction: revenue overstated.
- **How to implement:** Two price index sets. p_DA_h for h in 1..24 applied to both HH periods in the hour; p_ID30_t for t in 1..48. Constrain the day-ahead decision variables to be hour-constant: e_DA_{2h-1} = e_DA_{2h}. Allow half-hourly deviation only in variables settled against p_ID30_t or the intraday continuous index.
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'Wholesale trading: what are the different markets and how do they work', https://modoenergy.com/research/en/wholesale-trading-markets-explainer-gb-n2ex-epex-dayahead-intraday
- *Source:* Nord Pool, 'About the N2EX Day Ahead Auction', https://support.nordpoolgroup.com/support/solutions/articles/8000088463-about-the-n2ex-day-ahead-auction
- *Source:* EPEX SPOT, 'Focus GB', https://www.epexspot.com/en/focus-gb

#### D-141 · MUST · Implement the GB single imbalance price correctly: one cash-out price per settlement period applied to both long and short positions, derived from a PAR volume of 1 MWh, with a Reserve Scarcity Price element using VoLL of £6,000/MWh, and NO cap on the resulting price.

- **Why:** P305 moved GB to a single marginal cash-out price with PAR reduced from 500 MWh to 50 MWh on 5 November 2015 and to 1 MWh on 1 November 2018. Ofgem's P443 decision (20 October 2023) confirms VoLL is administratively set at £6,000/MWh within the BSC (Section T 1.12.2) and explicitly rejected capping the imbalance price, noting NGESO interconnector actions priced up to £9,500/MWh in July 2022.
- **Error if omitted:** A model using a dual price, a 50 MWh PAR, or a capped cash-out is running pre-2018 GB rules. It will understate the tail of the imbalance distribution and therefore both the risk of being short and the value of being long in scarcity.
- **How to implement:** SIP_t = marginal price of the most expensive 1 MWh of NIV-tagged system actions in period t, with RSP_t = LoLP_t * VoLL + (1-LoLP_t) * SRMC where VoLL = 6000 £/MWh, included in the price stack. Imbalance cashflow = (physical_delivered_t - contracted_t) * SIP_t, same price both directions. No cap term.
- *Source:* Ofgem, 'Authority Decision to Reject BSC modification P443: To Cap NGESO Interconnector Trades at the Value of Lost Load', 20 October 2023, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/sites/default/files/2023-10/P443%20-%20Authority%20Decision%20to%20Reject%20Balancing%20and%20Settlement%20Code%20modification.pdf
- *Source:* Ofgem, 'P305 BSC acceptance letter', 29 March 2015, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/sites/default/files/docs/2015/04/p305d_1.pdf
- *Source:* Ofgem, 'Analysis of the first phase of the Electricity Balancing Significant Code Review', 2018, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/sites/default/files/docs/2018/08/analysis_of_the_first_phase_of_the_electricity_balancing_significant_code_review_as_final_version_publication.pdf

#### D-142 · MUST · For a behind-the-meter asset, gate wholesale market access on P415 eligibility and model ONLY the Deviation Volume as tradable — never nameplate power.

- **Why:** P415 (Ofgem decision 6 October 2023, implemented 7 November 2024) created Deviation Volumes as a new settlement volume type so a Virtual Lead Party can trade a consumer's flexibility on the wholesale market without the supplier. But the tradable quantity is the difference between the baseline and actual metered consumption, not the battery's rating. Orkestra states it directly: you can only trade what is different from what the battery would do anyway.
- **Error if omitted:** This is the single largest structural overstatement available in a BTM model. Assuming a 1 MW BTM battery can sell 1 MW into the day-ahead market ignores the entire settlement architecture and can inflate wholesale revenue several-fold.
- **How to implement:** DV_t = Baseline_t - Metered_t, and constrain the wholesale traded volume w_t such that |w_t| <= |DV_t|. Wholesale revenue = sum_t DV_t * p_t. Battery power/energy constraints bind on Metered_t, not on w_t directly.
- *Source:* Ofgem, 'Decision on BSC P415: Facilitating access to wholesale markets for flexibility dispatched by Virtual Lead Parties', 6 October 2023, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/sites/default/files/2023-10/Ofgem%20decision%20P415%20'Facilitating%20Access%20to%20Wholesale%20Markets%20for%20Flexibility%20Dispatched%20by%20VLPs_0.pdf
- *Source:* Orkestra, 'P415 and batteries', https://www.orkestra.energy/post/p415-and-batteries
- *Source:* Axle Energy, 'P415: how to wholesale', https://www.axle.energy/blog/p415-how-to-wholesale

#### D-143 · MUST · Implement the P415 BL01 baseline explicitly, including its eligibility rules, and test whether the dispatch schedule the optimiser produces would destroy the baseline it depends on.

- **Why:** Elexon's default BL01 baseline is the straight average of the same day-type over 10 working days or 4 non-working days, plus an in-day adjustment for conditions up to 3 hours before gate closure. At least 5 eligible days are required within any 60-day window, and days on which flexibility was delivered are excluded as event days. High-frequency dispatch therefore self-disqualifies the site from having a valid baseline.
- **Error if omitted:** A model that dispatches daily and simultaneously assumes a valid baseline is internally inconsistent — it is claiming a wholesale revenue that the settlement rules would refuse. Direction: revenue overstated, and the failure is binary rather than marginal.
- **How to implement:** Baseline_t = UnadjustedBaseline_t + InDayAdjustment_t, where UnadjustedBaseline_t = mean over the 10 most recent eligible working days (4 for non-working) of metered demand in period t, and InDayAdjustment is computed from the hours up to GateClosure - 3h. Add a hard constraint: count of non-event days in any rolling 60 days >= 5. If the MILP violates it, the wholesale revenue for those days must be zeroed. Ofgem's own CBA notes the method is designed for large industrial consumers with consistent, predictable demand — flag any site whose HH load CV exceeds a threshold as baseline-unreliable.
- *Source:* Gridcog, 'P415: what is the baselining methodology?', https://www.gridcog.com/blog/p415-what-is-the-baselining-methodology
- *Source:* Ofgem, 'Decision on BSC P415', 6 October 2023, baselining section, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/sites/default/files/2023-10/Ofgem%20decision%20P415%20'Facilitating%20Access%20to%20Wholesale%20Markets%20for%20Flexibility%20Dispatched%20by%20VLPs_0.pdf
- *Source:* Orkestra, 'P415 baselining and deviation volumes', https://www.orkestra.energy/post/p415-baselining-and-deviation-volumes

#### D-144 · MUST · Gate P415 participation on the host MPAN being half-hourly settled, and track the MHHS migration timetable as a live eligibility risk.

- **Why:** Axle Energy states that devices must sit behind half-hourly settled meters for P415 trading, because suppliers must be purchasing against actual metered data rather than a profile. Elexon began migrating MPANs to half-hourly settlement on 22 October 2025 and expects roughly 80% of meters onboarded by October 2026.
- **Error if omitted:** Crediting P415 wholesale revenue from year 1 at a site whose meter is not yet HH settled books revenue that cannot be settled. Direction: revenue overstated and mis-timed.
- **How to implement:** Boolean gate hh_settled(site, date); wholesale_revenue_t = 0 while false. Model the migration date as an input with a probability distribution rather than a certainty.
- *Source:* Axle Energy, 'P415: how to wholesale', https://www.axle.energy/blog/p415-how-to-wholesale
- *Source:* Elexon, 'Market-wide Half-Hourly Settlement reaches major milestone', 24 September 2025, https://www.elexon.co.uk/2025/09/24/market-wide-half-hourly-settlement-reaches-major-milestone/

#### D-145 · MUST · If the BTM battery is to be settled separately from the host site (as a Secondary BM Unit or for asset-metered VLP participation), require and cost COP11-compliant asset metering under P375.

- **Why:** P375, implemented 30 June 2022, allows individual assets behind the grid boundary point to be settled using secondary asset meters complying with Code of Practice 11, so a behind-the-meter battery can participate in the BM and balancing services without its energy 'mingling' with site load and attracting imbalance across the whole site.
- **Error if omitted:** A model that assumes BM or VLP participation for a BTM asset without provisioning asset metering has omitted both a capex line and a legal precondition. Direction: capex understated and revenue access overstated.
- **How to implement:** Add a capex/opex line for COP11 asset metering plus HHDC/AMVLP/AMMOA registration, and make BM/VLP revenue lines conditional on that line being present.
- *Source:* Freeths, 'P375 metering behind the boundary point: a game changer for electricity asset co-location', 14 July 2022, https://www.freeths.co.uk/2022/07/14/p375-metering-behind-the-boundary-point-a-game-changer-for-electricity-asset-co-location/
- *Source:* Ofgem, 'P375: Settlement of Secondary BM Units using metering behind the site boundary point', https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/publications/p375-settlement-secondary-bm-units-using-metering-behind-site-boundary-point-0
- *Source:* Axle Energy, 'P415: how to wholesale', https://www.axle.energy/blog/p415-how-to-wholesale

#### D-146 · MUST · Model avoided import cost and exported energy revenue as two distinct, differently priced cashflows, and never let a discharged MWh earn both.

- **Why:** A MWh discharged behind the meter to displace site load avoids the full delivered retail cost of that MWh — commodity plus network, policy and levy components. A MWh exported earns only the wholesale/market price for exported energy. In GB these differ by a very large margin: Modo's own tabulation of import charges on a battery without a generation licence runs to roughly £60-80/MWh of non-commodity cost on top of the wholesale price.
- **Error if omitted:** Valuing all discharge at the wholesale price massively understates BTM value; valuing all discharge at the retail tariff massively overstates it once site load is exhausted. Both are common and both are fatal to the underwriting.
- **How to implement:** Split discharge: d_t = d_onsite_t + d_export_t, with d_onsite_t <= site_load_t. Revenue_t = d_onsite_t * tariff_delivered_t + d_export_t * p_export_t, where tariff_delivered_t includes the avoidable elements only (do not credit avoidance of fixed standing/capacity charges).
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'Electricity import charges for battery energy storage', https://modoenergy.com/research/en/electricity-import-charges-battery-energy-storage
- *Source:* Gridcog, 'The economics of behind-the-meter battery storage part 1: reducing your energy bill', https://www.gridcog.com/blog/the-economics-of-behind-the-meter-battery-storage-part-1-reducing-your-energy-bill

#### D-147 · MUST · Make export physically and contractually conditional. Require evidence of an export MPAN AND a DNO connection agreement permitting export at the modelled MW before any export revenue is booked; otherwise hard-cap discharge at site load.

- **Why:** An export MPAN is issued by the DNO and is the settlement identity without which exported energy is delivered to the grid unpaid. Separately, many GB C&I connections are export-limited or zero-export, enforced by a G100 export limitation scheme, and G99 is the connection procedure that determines the permitted export capacity.
- **Error if omitted:** This is the most expensive single omission available in a BTM model. A 2 MW battery at a site with a 500 kW average load and a zero-export connection has its discharge constrained to roughly 25% of nameplate in most half-hours. Modelling it as a merchant exporter overstates energy revenue by a multiple, not a percentage.
- **How to implement:** Boolean export_permitted(site) and scalar export_limit_MW. Constraint: d_export_t <= export_permitted * export_limit_MW * 0.5 (MWh per HH), and d_export_t = 0 when export_permitted is false. Similarly cap charging at import_limit_MW where a G100 import limitation applies.
- *Source:* National Grid Electricity Distribution, 'Customer export limitation schemes', https://connections.nationalgrid.co.uk/customer-export-limitation-schemes
- *Source:* Sunsave, 'Export MPAN explained', https://www.sunsave.energy/solar-panels-advice/exporting-to-the-grid/export-mpan
- *Source:* National Grid, 'G99 Connection Procedures Guidance Document', https://connections.nationalgrid.co.uk/downloads/24747

#### D-148 · MUST · If a licence-exempt sleeved supply structure is relied upon to avoid EMR levies, test it against the Class A thresholds and the P442 arrangements, and model the ESNA reporting obligation as an opex line.

- **Why:** The Class A small supplier exemption permits a maximum of 5 MW total supplied with no more than 2.5 MW to domestic consumers, is technology-neutral and requires no pre-approval but does require self-assessment; the limit applies to the amount supplied, not consumed on site. BSC modification P442, approved May 2024 and implemented July 2025, excludes exempt volumes from CfD and Capacity Market levies and introduced Exempt Supply Notification Agents to validate and submit the data.
- **Error if omitted:** Either the model credits levy avoidance the structure does not qualify for (revenue overstated, and a regulatory finding on the site), or it ignores a legitimate £30-40/MWh saving available to a sub-5 MW C&I arrangement (revenue understated).
- **How to implement:** Boolean class_a_eligible = (annual_supplied_MW <= 5) AND (domestic_supplied_MW <= 2.5). If true, zero the CfD and CM levy terms in charge_cost_t and add an annual ESNA/reporting opex. If false, apply full levies. Since these are size-thresholded, the eligibility test must be re-run in every sizing iteration of the optimisation, not fixed once.
- *Source:* Foot Anstey, 'A new era for licence-exempt supply: P442 and Class A guidance updates', https://www.footanstey.com/our-insights/articles-news/a-new-era-for-licence-exempt-supply-p442-and-class-a-guidance-updates/
- *Source:* DESNZ, 'Electricity licence exemptions guidance', https://assets.publishing.service.gov.uk/media/6967bf7db122b8f5f68d0ddd/electricity-licence-exemptions-guidance.pdf
- *Source:* GOV.UK, 'Electricity licence exemptions', https://www.gov.uk/guidance/electricity-licence-exemptions

#### D-149 · MUST · Benchmark any assumed floor or toll level against observed GB transactions, and do not assume a BTM sub-5 MW asset can obtain grid-scale terms.

- **Why:** Two GB reference points exist. Gresham House / Octopus, June 2024: 568 MW / 920 MWh, 2-year term, £43m per year, which Modo computes as £57k/MW/year. Gresham House, July 2025: 789 MW secured minimum annual contracted revenues of £35m over a 10-year tenor with Statkraft Markets and Markel Bermuda (via Nephila), approximately £44.4k/MW/year, with merchant upside retained above the floor. Both are transmission-connected, portfolio-scale deals.
- **Error if omitted:** An underwriting that assumes a floor for a single 1 MW BTM asset at portfolio pricing is assuming a counterparty appetite that the published deal set does not evidence. Direction: contracted revenue and therefore debt capacity overstated.
- **How to implement:** Input floor_level_kGBP_per_MW_yr with a documented comparable. Apply a size/liquidity discount for assets below the observed transaction scale, and flag the assumption in the underwriting pack rather than burying it.
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'Battery tolling announced: Gresham House and Octopus Energy', June 2024, https://modoenergy.com/research/battery-tolling-announced-gresham-house-octopus-energy-june-2024
- *Source:* Gresham House, 'Long-term floor agreements agreed on 789MW of battery projects', July 2025, https://greshamhouse.com/news-media/long-term-floor-agreements-agreed-on-789mw-of-battery-projects/
- *Source:* Energy-Storage.news, 'UK: Gresham House signs 789MW floor deals, Octopus toll to expire', https://www.energy-storage.news/uk-gresham-house-signs-789mw-floor-deals-octopus-toll-to-expire/

#### D-150 · MUST · Build the revenue stack from published, dated GB benchmark splits and require the model's own split to sit within the observed historical range — including allowing the wholesale line to be negative.

- **Why:** On Modo's ME BESS GB 2-hour index the annual splits were: 2023 total £75.6k/MW/yr (wholesale 31%, balancing 4%, ancillary net 46%, capacity 19%); 2024 total £60.7k (45%/27%/5%/22%); 2025 total £80.1k (39%/24%/28%/9%); Jan-Apr 2026 annualised £70.0k (14%/53%/21%/12%). The wholesale line was NEGATIVE in February 2026 (-£6,247/MW/yr) and March 2026 (-£1,694/MW/yr) while balancing revenue reached £64,943/MW/yr in March 2026.
- **Error if omitted:** A model whose wholesale share is pinned at a single number is calibrated to a market state that persisted for at most one year. A model that constrains wholesale revenue to be non-negative cannot reproduce two of the last fifteen months of GB history.
- **How to implement:** Store the monthly stack as a resampling pool. Assert that modelled wholesale_share sits in [10%, 50%] and balancing_share in [4%, 55%] over a rolling 12-month window, and permit wholesale_revenue < 0 in the solver output without treating it as an error.
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'GB BESS revenue stack evolution' chart (ME BESS GB 2H Index), monthly data Jan 2023 - Apr 2026, https://modoenergy.github.io/modo-charts/charts/europe/gb/geo-foundation-bess-revenue/chart-e-gb-revenue-stack-evolution.html
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'Revenue Stack - GB BESS Benchmark' chart, ME BESS GB revenues since Mar-25, https://modoenergy.github.io/modo-charts/charts/europe/gb/bess-benchmark-monthly/gb-benchmark-1-revenue-stack.html

#### D-151 · MUST · Permit and model negative wholesale prices, including paying to charge, and check whether the site's actual supply contract passes them through.

- **Why:** GB recorded 176 hours of negative power prices in 2024, with Modo forecasting over 200 in 2025. Negative prices are the mechanism by which the TB1 spread persists even as it has fallen 53% since 2021. But a BTM site on a fixed retail tariff does not see them — Modo notes small sites may lack direct hourly wholesale price exposure due to hedging or fixed-price PPAs.
- **Error if omitted:** Two opposite errors. Clipping prices at zero removes a real source of charging value. Assuming a fixed-tariff BTM site captures negative prices credits a signal the site cannot see under its supply contract.
- **How to implement:** Allow p_t < 0 in the price series with no floor. Add a boolean passthrough(site) which, when false, replaces p_t with the contracted tariff for the avoided-import calculation while retaining p_t for any P415 Deviation Volume traded through a VLP.
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'GB battery energy storage markets: 2024 year in review', https://modoenergy.com/research/en/gb-battery-energy-storage-markets-2024-year-in-review-great-britain-wholesale-balancing-mechanism-frequency-response-reserve
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'Top-bottom spread revenue benchmark', https://modoenergy.com/research/en/top-bottom-spread-revenue-benchmark-battery-energy-storage-sytems-gb-europe-spain-germany-solar-2025
- *Source:* Modo Energy documentation, 'Small BESS sites', https://docs.modoenergy.com/pages/apr-2026/uk-europe/gb/small-bess-sites/

#### D-152 · MUST · Do not carry any pre-2023 GB assumption about frequency response into the model without re-basing it, and do not use pre-2019 imbalance rules.

- **Why:** Ancillary services fell from roughly 87% of the GB stack in 2020-2022 to roughly 33% gross by 2026, and the net ancillary contribution was negative in five months of 2024 because Dynamic Regulation High import costs exceeded the positive ancillary contribution. Quick Reserve (PQR) launched 3 December 2024 and an ABSVD rule change in September 2025 allowed non-BM units to compete in Quick Reserve. On the imbalance side, PAR was 500 MWh before November 2015 and 50 MWh before November 2018; it has been 1 MWh since.
- **Error if omitted:** A model built on 2019 GB literature will assume a dominant, high-margin frequency response revenue line that no longer exists, and a materially damped cash-out price that no longer exists. Both errors point the same way: an overstated, falsely stable revenue profile.
- **How to implement:** Version-stamp every market assumption with the rule set it belongs to and refuse to load an assumption whose vintage predates the last relevant code change. Maintain a table mapping each revenue line to its governing modification (P305 for cash-out, P375 for asset metering, P415 for VLP wholesale access, P442 for exempt supply levies).
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'How does battery energy storage make money?', https://modoenergy.com/research/en/how-does-battery-energy-storage-make-money
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'GB BESS revenue stack evolution' chart methodology note, https://modoenergy.github.io/modo-charts/charts/europe/gb/geo-foundation-bess-revenue/chart-e-gb-revenue-stack-evolution.html
- *Source:* Ofgem, 'Analysis of the first phase of the Electricity Balancing Significant Code Review', 2018, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/sites/default/files/docs/2018/08/analysis_of_the_first_phase_of_the_electricity_balancing_significant_code_review_as_final_version_publication.pdf
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'GB battery energy storage: de-risking returns, Q3 2025', https://modoenergy.com/research/en/gb-battery-energy-storage-derisking-returns-revenues-great-britain-offtake-ldes-p462-q3-2025

#### D-153 · MUST · Require genuine half-hourly site load data (or a defensible synthesis) as an input gate, because for a BTM asset the load IS the export constraint and the baseline.

- **Why:** For a behind-the-meter battery, site load determines (a) how much discharge can be monetised as avoided import when export is limited, (b) the BL01 baseline against which P415 Deviation Volumes are computed, and (c) the imbalance exposure from forecast error. Ofgem's P415 CBA notes baselining is better designed for large industrial consumers with consistent, predictable demand — meaning load predictability is itself a revenue driver.
- **Error if omitted:** Without HH load the model cannot compute the export cap, cannot compute the baseline, and cannot compute forecast error. Any revenue number produced is unfalsifiable. Direction: unbounded error.
- **How to implement:** Hard input gate: reject an underwriting run lacking 8,760x2 half-hourly site consumption. Where synthesised, record the synthesis method and propagate a load-uncertainty band into the P90. Compute and report the load coefficient of variation as a baseline-reliability indicator.
- *Source:* Ofgem, 'Decision on BSC P415', 6 October 2023, baselining section, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/sites/default/files/2023-10/Ofgem%20decision%20P415%20'Facilitating%20Access%20to%20Wholesale%20Markets%20for%20Flexibility%20Dispatched%20by%20VLPs_0.pdf
- *Source:* Gridcog, 'P415: what is the baselining methodology?', https://www.gridcog.com/blog/p415-what-is-the-baselining-methodology
- *Source:* Gridcog, 'The economics of behind-the-meter battery storage part 2', https://www.gridcog.com/blog/the-economics-of-behind-the-meter-battery-storage-part-2-earning-value-in-energy-markets

#### D-154 · MUST · Enforce a no-simultaneous-charge-and-discharge condition, or prove from the price and charge-cost data that it cannot pay.

- **Why:** With a delivered charging cost that includes £60-80/MWh of levies at a BTM site, and with negative wholesale prices occurring for 176 hours in 2024, the sign of the charge-leg cost can invert. When the effective charging price is negative and the discharge price is positive, an LP relaxation without a binary can find a spurious simultaneous charge/discharge solution that manufactures revenue from round-trip losses.
- **Error if omitted:** Physically impossible dispatch and fabricated revenue, concentrated exactly in the negative-price hours that a GB model must be able to represent. Direction: revenue overstated, and the artefact is easy to miss because it appears only in a minority of periods.
- **How to implement:** Binary u_t per settlement period with c_t <= P_max * u_t and d_t <= P_max * (1 - u_t). If the binary is dropped for tractability, add a post-solve assertion that c_t * d_t = 0 for all t and fail the run if violated.
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'Electricity import charges for battery energy storage', https://modoenergy.com/research/en/electricity-import-charges-battery-energy-storage
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'GB battery energy storage markets: 2024 year in review', https://modoenergy.com/research/en/gb-battery-energy-storage-markets-2024-year-in-review-great-britain-wholesale-balancing-mechanism-frequency-response-reserve

#### D-155 · MUST · Under P415, model the supplier compensation flow correctly: under the directed Proposed Solution the cost is MUTUALISED across suppliers and priced using Ofgem's Price Cap Methodology, so the VLP does NOT pay it directly — but the rejected Alternative Solution would have charged it to the VLP.

- **Why:** Ofgem directed the Proposed Solution on 6 October 2023, under which suppliers commercially impacted by VLP action are compensated and the compensation costs are mutualised amongst suppliers, at a price representing average supplier sourcing costs per the Price Cap Methodology. Ofgem also flagged that it will monitor the resulting uneven playing field and expects industry to progress changes.
- **Error if omitted:** Charging the VLP a supplier compensation cost it does not currently bear understates BTM wholesale revenue; assuming the mutualisation is permanent ignores Ofgem's explicit statement that it expects industry to revisit it. Both should be modelled.
- **How to implement:** Parameter vlp_pays_supplier_compensation (default false, matching the implemented Proposed Solution), with a downside scenario setting it true and pricing it at the Ofgem PCM wholesale sourcing cost per Deviation Volume MWh.
- *Source:* Ofgem, 'Decision on BSC P415: Facilitating access to wholesale markets for flexibility dispatched by Virtual Lead Parties', 6 October 2023, https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/sites/default/files/2023-10/Ofgem%20decision%20P415%20'Facilitating%20Access%20to%20Wholesale%20Markets%20for%20Flexibility%20Dispatched%20by%20VLPs_0.pdf

#### D-156 · MUST · Enforce a single shared power and energy budget across ALL services in every half hour: the sum of energy-market charge/discharge, peak-shaving discharge, and any capacity reserved for frequency response or Capacity Market obligation must not exceed the inverter rating or the stored energy. Never compute service values in separate passes and add them.

- **Why:** GB rules treat stacking as simultaneous delivery of two or more services from the same MW. NESO explicitly prohibits stacking two Dynamic Frequency Response services (DC+DM, DM+DR) and permits a single DFR with the BM only under conditions that reserve capability. The naive sum of standalone optimisations is not physically deliverable.
- **Error if omitted:** Revenue is overstated by the whole of the smaller conflicting stream. In the limit, a model that separately credits full peak shaving and full DC availability on the same 1 MW/1 MWh asset double-counts 100% of one of them. Direction: always overstates.
- **How to implement:** For each period t: P_dis[t] + P_ch[t] + R_up_reserved[t] <= P_rated and P_ch[t] + R_dn_reserved[t] <= P_rated; and E[t] >= R_up_reserved[t] * T_service (T_service = 0.25 h for DC, 0.5 h for DM, 1.0 h for DR) and (E_max - E[t]) >= R_dn_reserved[t] * T_service. Add mutual exclusion binaries: y_DC[b] + y_DM[b] + y_DR[b] <= 1 for each 4-hour EFA block b.
- *Source:* National Grid ESO, 'Unlocking Stacking of BOAs with Frequency Response Services', https://www.neso.energy/document/184466/download
- *Source:* NESO, 'Dynamic Response Services – Provider Guidance', v14, June 2026, https://www.neso.energy/document/276606/download
- *Source:* Seward, W., Qadrdan, M., Jenkins, N., 'Revenue stacking for behind the meter battery storage in energy and ancillary services markets', Electric Power Systems Research 211 (2022) 108292 / PSCC 2022, https://pscc-central.epfl.ch/repo/papers/2022/19890.pdf

#### D-157 · MUST · Gate ALL frequency-response revenue (DC/DM/DR) on an eligibility test: the response unit must offer at least 1 MW, orders must be whole MW, and aggregation is only permitted across assets behind the SAME GSP Group. A sub-1 MW standalone BTM asset must be credited zero DC/DM/DR revenue unless an aggregation route within its GSP Group is explicitly modelled, with the aggregator's margin deducted.

- **Why:** NESO Provider Guidance states a minimum response capacity of 1 MW and a maximum of 100 MW per response unit, that sell orders may only be submitted in whole MWs, and that aggregation is possible only when assets sit behind the same GSP Group (subject to technical assurance).
- **Error if omitted:** For the 0.1–1 MW segment — the bulk of BTM C&I — the model invents a revenue stream that does not exist. GB BESS frequency-response revenue ran at ~£30k/MW/yr in October 2025 (Modo), so crediting it to a 500 kW asset overstates revenue by ~£15k/yr, typically a large share of a small project's margin.
- **How to implement:** Boolean gate: eligible_FR = (P_rated_MW >= 1.0) OR (aggregation_agreement_exists AND same_GSP_Group). Round contracted volume down: V_contract = floor(P_available_MW). Apply aggregator fee as a percentage haircut on gross FR revenue and document it as an assumption.
- *Source:* NESO, 'Dynamic Response Services – Provider Guidance', v14, June 2026, slide 6, https://www.neso.energy/document/276606/download
- *Source:* Modo Energy, 'ME BESS GB: Revenues climb to £77k/MW/year in October 2025', published 6 November 2025, https://modoenergy.com/research/en/battery-energy-storage-revenues-gb-october-2025-record-balancing-mechanism-dispatch-rates

#### D-158 · MUST · Do NOT model frequency-response revenue as contracted MW × clearing price × hours. Apply the settlement formula including the binary availability factor F (which is ZERO for an entire settlement period if the unit is unavailable for any part of it) and the performance factor K derived from high-frequency error data.

- **Why:** The NESO service terms settlement value is S_aij = Round((P_aj − ((1−K_aij) × PF_aj)) × V_aij × 0.5 × F_aij, 2). F_ij is 0 where there is ANY period of unavailability within settlement period j. For a Low service, availability is computed from 20 Hz data (36,000 rows per settlement period) and F = 0 if availability < 99.9%.
- **Error if omitted:** Overstates frequency-response revenue. A single brief comms dropout or SOC excursion in a half hour zeroes the whole half hour's availability payment, and sustained delivery error above the K-factor's upper bound (B = 0.07 for DC) zeroes the performance component. Models that ignore this quote a theoretical maximum no operator achieves.
- **How to implement:** Revenue_FR[j] = P_clear[j] * V_MW * 0.5 * F[j] * K[j], with F[j] ∈ {0,1} sampled from an assumed availability distribution (e.g. F=1 with probability p_avail) and K[j] drawn from a performance distribution. For DC, k_j = 1 if E < 0.03; k_j = 1 − (E − 0.03)/(0.07 − 0.03) if 0.03 <= E <= 0.07; k_j = 0 if E > 0.07. Underwrite P90 on the low tail of p_avail.
- *Source:* NESO, 'Dynamic Response Services – Provider Guidance', v14, June 2026, slides 14 and 19, https://www.neso.energy/document/276606/download

#### D-159 · MUST · Treat the Transmission Demand Residual (TDR) as a FIXED £/site/day charge that the battery cannot reduce, allocated by band and locked for the whole price control period. Explicitly model the risk that raising MIC to accommodate the battery pushes the site into a higher (more expensive) band.

- **Why:** Post-TCR, the residual element of demand charges is levied on a £/site/day basis with sites allocated into bands, and 'users remain in band for the duration of price control'. Bands are set by MIC (kVA) at LV-with-MIC, HV and EHV. Triad-based residual recovery ended: the last Triad period was February 2023 and from April 2023 the TDR became a fixed £/site/day varying by meter type and band.
- **Error if omitted:** Two errors, both in the optimistic direction. (a) Crediting the battery with avoided TNUoS residual — historically the largest TNUoS component — is pure fiction post-April 2023. (b) Ignoring the band-jump risk understates cost: RIIO3 HV Band 2 begins above 500 kVA and Band 3 above 1,100 kVA, so adding a 1 MW battery to an 800 kVA HV site can move it two bands.
- **How to implement:** TDR_annual = tariff_band(voltage_class, MIC_kVA, price_control) * 365, treated as a constant in the objective (it drops out of dispatch optimisation) but as a step function in the SIZING loop: MIC_new = MIC_old + P_rated_kVA if the connection must be uprated; re-evaluate band and add ΔTDR to project cost. RIIO2 (2021/22–2025/26) LV-MIC thresholds: Band1 ≤80, Band2 >80–150, Band3 >150–231, Band4 >231 kVA. RIIO3 (2026/27–2030/31): LV-MIC ≤90 / >90–150 / >150–250 / >250; HV ≤500 / >500–1,100 / >1,100–2,000 / >2,000; EHV ≤3,500 / >3,500–11,000 / >11,000–20,000 / >20,000 kVA.
- *Source:* NESO, 'Transmission Demand Residual (TDR) Guidance Document', Tables 1a and 1b, https://www.neso.energy/document/288186/download
- *Source:* Ofgem, 'Targeted Charging Review: decision and impact assessment', 18 December 2019 (decision confirmed 21 November 2021 per NESO guidance), https://www.ofgem.gov.uk/decision/targeted-charging-review-decision-and-impact-assessment
- *Source:* Drax, 'Triads: How TNUoS charges are changing', https://energy.drax.com/insights/end-of-triads-tnuos-charges-changing/

#### D-160 · MUST · Do not allow the model to escape residual charges by declaring the host a 'Non-Final Demand Site'. Apply a hard test: the exemption requires a Single Site where ONLY electricity storage and/or generation occurs, with an export MPAN and an import MPAN whose metering measures only the storage/generation. A C&I host with a factory, warehouse or office on the same connection is a Final Demand Site and pays the full residual.

- **Why:** The DCUSA Non-Final Demand Site certificate of compliance requires the property to be a Single Site at which either or both Electricity Storage and/or Electricity Generation occurs, with metering that 'only measures export from Electricity Storage and/or Electricity Generation and import for or directly relating to Electricity Storage'. NESO's TDR guidance states the same for transmission: 'if there is anything else at that site such as a factory or a data centre then that whole site would be liable for the charge'.
- **Error if omitted:** Overstates savings by the full residual (fixed £/site/day at both DUoS and TNUoS). This is a common and expensive error imported from front-of-meter storage models where the exemption genuinely applies.
- **How to implement:** Flag site_type ∈ {final_demand, non_final_demand, mixed}. Set non_final_demand = TRUE only if there is no non-storage load behind the same connection agreement AND separate storage-only MPANs exist. Otherwise residual_charges_avoidable = 0. Note the certification is not automatic: 'all sites are liable for the TDR unless they opt out' and re-certification may be required on any capacity change.
- *Source:* London Power Networks plc, 'Use of System Charging Statement', effective 1 April 2026, v1.2, Section 10 and Appendix 3, https://media.umbraco.io/ukpn-cms/h5ahq0jp/london-power-networks-lc14-statement-2026-v1-2.pdf
- *Source:* NESO, 'Transmission Demand Residual (TDR) Guidance Document', pages 5 and 9–10, https://www.neso.energy/document/288186/download

#### D-161 · MUST · Model DUoS capacity charges against the contractual MIC/MEC, not against measured peak demand, and apply the rule that MIC may be reduced only once in any 12-month period and not at all within 12 months of the MIC being agreed. Do not credit capacity-charge savings in year 1.

- **Why:** DNO charging statements state that chargeable capacity is the MIC/MEC agreed at connection, that following such an agreement 'no reduction in MIC/MEC will be allowed for a 12 month period', and that reductions 'may only be permitted once in a 12 month period'. They also warn that once reduced, 'the original capacity may not be available in the future without the need for network reinforcement and associated charges'.
- **Error if omitted:** Overstates savings and mis-times cash flow. A model that converts peak-kW reduction directly into p/kVA/day savings from month 1 books revenue that cannot legally be realised for at least a year, and ignores the option cost of surrendering headroom.
- **How to implement:** Capacity_charge[t] = MIC_contracted_kVA * rate_p_per_kVA_per_day. Allow MIC_contracted to step down only at t >= 12 months after connection agreement, at most once per rolling 12 months, and only to a level justified by observed maximum demand. Add a project-risk flag if the reduced MIC would be insufficient for forecast load growth.
- *Source:* London Power Networks plc, 'Use of System Charging Statement', effective 1 April 2026, v1.2, paras 2.37–2.40, https://media.umbraco.io/ukpn-cms/h5ahq0jp/london-power-networks-lc14-statement-2026-v1-2.pdf

#### D-162 · MUST · Model the exceeded-capacity charge with the published apparent-power formula, computed for EVERY half hour with the maximum in the billing period applied — and check that battery charging cannot itself trigger it.

- **Why:** Exceeded capacity is charged on the excess over MIC for the full duration of the billing period in which the breach occurs. The DNO formula is apparent-power based, so a battery charging at high current with poor power factor can breach MIC even when active-power import looks acceptable.
- **Error if omitted:** Understates cost and, worse, hides a failure mode where the optimiser charges the battery hard in cheap green-band hours and creates an exceeded-capacity event that costs more than the arbitrage earned. Direction: understates cost, and can invert the sign of a dispatch decision.
- **How to implement:** Demand exceeded capacity = max( 2 × sqrt( AI² + max(RI, RE)² ) − MIC , 0 ), where AI = active import (kWh in the half hour), RI = reactive import (kVArh), RE = reactive export (kVArh), MIC in kVA. Compute for every half hour; apply the maximum over the billing period. Generation exceeded capacity uses AE (active export) against MEC. Add MILP constraint: 2*sqrt((P_load[t]+P_ch[t])² + Q[t]²) <= MIC (linearise with a polygonal approximation of the apparent-power circle).
- *Source:* London Power Networks plc, 'Use of System Charging Statement', effective 1 April 2026, v1.2, paras 2.41–2.45, https://media.umbraco.io/ukpn-cms/h5ahq0jp/london-power-networks-lc14-statement-2026-v1-2.pdf

#### D-163 · MUST · Model reactive power and excess-reactive charges explicitly, and model the battery inverter's reactive behaviour. Do not assume unity power factor.

- **Why:** DNOs charge for reactive units where an individual HH MPAN's kVArh exceeds 33% of its kWh in any half hour — equivalent to an average power factor of 0.95. The chargeable quantity is calculated per half hour and summed over the billing period. Under EDCM there is no separate reactive component but poor power factor increases unit charges and raises the kVA needed for the same kW.
- **Error if omitted:** Two-sided error. Ignoring it understates cost at sites with inductive load; ignoring the inverter's ability to supply VArs forgoes a real saving. Either way the model is wrong about the direction of a design decision (whether to specify a 4-quadrant inverter).
- **How to implement:** Demand chargeable kVArh[t] = max( max(RI[t], RE[t]) − sqrt(1/0.95² − 1) × AI[t] , 0 ), summed over the billing period and priced at the p/kVArh rate. If the inverter is 4-quadrant, allow Q_batt[t] as a decision variable bounded by sqrt(S_inv² − P_batt[t]²) and subtract it from RI[t]; charge the resulting apparent-power headroom loss against active-power capability.
- *Source:* London Power Networks plc, 'Use of System Charging Statement', effective 1 April 2026, v1.2, paras 2.48–2.53 and Appendix 2 paras 1.17–1.21, https://media.umbraco.io/ukpn-cms/h5ahq0jp/london-power-networks-lc14-statement-2026-v1-2.pdf

#### D-164 · MUST · Model DUoS red/amber/green volumetric avoidance as a genuine, dispatchable saving — this is one of the few network charges a BTM battery CAN reduce — but use the correct DNO-specific time bands and current-year rates, and re-derive the value each charging year rather than escalating.

- **Why:** DUoS unit charges are p/kWh by time band and are avoided by reducing import in the red band. Red band windows differ by DNO (examples: 16:00–19:30, 16:30–19:00, 16:00–19:00 on weekdays). The magnitude has been volatile: for a residual band 2 LV site-specific user, 2025/26 versus 2024/25 saw standing charges fall ~72% on average while capacity charges rose ~108% on average (up to +246% in Northern Powergrid Northeast) and unit rates rose ~30–40%, with overall DUoS down ~6% on average but ranging from +25% to −32% by region.
- **Error if omitted:** Both directions. Using a stale red-band rate understates the (currently rising) volumetric prize; using a generic national rate misallocates value between regions by tens of percent; using the wrong window shaves the wrong half hours entirely and delivers near-zero realised saving.
- **How to implement:** DUoS_cost = Σ_t import_kWh[t] × rate_band(DNO, band(t)); band(t) ∈ {red, amber, green} from the DNO's published Annex 1 time periods for the relevant LLFC/DUoS Tariff ID. The optimiser sees this directly as a time-varying import price adder. Under EDCM the analogue is the 'super-red' unit charge, which is positive for import and NEGATIVE for export — so an EHV site can be paid to export in super-red.
- *Source:* NUS Consulting Group, 'DUoS Charges (UK Mainland) 2025', https://www.nusconsulting.com/energy-news/uk-duos-charges-2025/
- *Source:* Drax, 'DUoS charges: 2025's box of frogs', https://energy.drax.com/intelligence/2025-duos-charges/
- *Source:* London Power Networks plc, 'Use of System Charging Statement', effective 1 April 2026, v1.2, paras 2.33 and Appendix 2 para 1.24(c), https://media.umbraco.io/ukpn-cms/h5ahq0jp/london-power-networks-lc14-statement-2026-v1-2.pdf
- *Source:* Seward, W., Qadrdan, M., Jenkins, N., PSCC 2022, Fig. 7 (worked DUoS red-band profile), https://pscc-central.epfl.ch/repo/papers/2022/19890.pdf

#### D-165 · MUST · Model the site's export limit as a physical constraint implemented by an EREC G100 Customer Limitation Scheme, including its control tolerance and its lock-out behaviour — not as a simple cap on exported kW.

- **Why:** EREC G100 Issue 2 Amd 2 requires CLS measurement/control tolerance of ±2% of the greater of MEL or MIL; a default maximum 1-minute response to bring flow back within limits (3 minutes for slow devices where the DNO agrees); entry to failed state (State 3) within 15 s of a detected failure; and lock-out to State 3 if there is a single excursion beyond 1 minute, or more than three excursions each >15 s and <1 minute in any 24 hours, or two consecutive >15 s excursions less than 10 minutes apart. For non-domestic installations, an excursion into State 3 'shall not be capable of being reset within 4 hours'.
- **Error if omitted:** Catastrophically overstates availability. An aggressive optimiser that sails at the export limit will trip the CLS; three trips in a day take the whole asset offline for 4 hours, zeroing peak shaving, arbitrage and (via the binary availability factor) any frequency-response payment for those settlement periods. Models that ignore this cannot reproduce observed downtime.
- **How to implement:** Constrain P_export[t] <= MEL × (1 − margin) with margin >= 0.02 plus a control-error allowance, and P_import[t] <= MIL × (1 − margin). Set an explicit State 1 operating limit inside the MEL/MIL as G100 §4.4.1 recommends, to avoid inadvertent State 2 entry. Model an availability penalty: if excursion_count_24h > 3 then asset_available = FALSE for the next 4 hours. Where MEL = 0 (non-exporting site) require reverse power protection instead.
